Where it started
Joshua wanted to assign the Read, Recall, Colour Activity to students and "make sure it works perfectly." That expanded into three tracks: building headless tooling to assign work and read student progress, discovering that student-facing copy lives in Postgres rather than the repo, and fixing a run of copy and contrast defects on the section briefing page. He granted standing pre-authorization to assign work to a single named student without asking first.
Decisions locked + what shipped

* RRC is an Activity, not a Drill. "Drill" is reserved for the MCQ engine per `src/features/activity-centre/CONTEXT.md`.
* Assign headlessly by calling the app's own `assignWork()`, never a reimplementation — assigning touches 7 tables plus an email function and rolls back on failure. `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\scripts\assign.ts`
* Read student progress — `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\scripts\student-work.ts`
* Two-way content sync, because the DB was ahead of the repo, not behind. Writes are opt-in (`--apply`); `--pull` follows the copy the most rows agree on, never the door row, which is stale. `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\scripts\content-push.ts`
* Rejected service_role auth despite Joshua selecting it: the key was never provisioned, admin login already existed, and service_role would hide RLS regressions. Scripts sign in as admin and warn if the key is present.
* Copy fixes, all live: "Recall Rephrase Colour" → real title; "Intake only…" → Joshua-approved option B; "Three beats" → "Three steps"; "22 pieces to read" → line deleted; door's retired "say it back"/"The Read" → "tell it back"/"The Recall"; 2 em dashes removed; notice cut from 3 facts to 1; long version collapsed behind `<details>`.
* A11y fixes, all live: focus rings on `.sl-start`/`.sl-brief` (were computing `outline-style: none` while `:focus-visible` matched), Back 43→44px, heading outline `H1 H2 H4×6` → `H1 H2 H2 H3×6`, `role="status"` removed from static copy, 18 `#fff` → `var(--rd-on-dark)`.
* Guards moved from components to content. Three rules leaked through one new page in a night because each was enforced per component.

# RRC: line-by-line marking against the answer key

The drill itself does not change. She still reads the whole stimulus, works
through it line by line out loud, then presses Space once to hide it and say the
whole thing back. One unbroken recording, one clock, nothing judged until the end.

What changes is everything after she finishes: the recording gets cut up to match
what was on screen, every line she rephrased gets marked against the answer key
for that line, and the Report Card shows the two side by side.

## What she gets at the end

For each question in the section:

- **Line by line.** The line as written, the key points that line contains, what
  she actually said about it, which points she carried and which she dropped, and
  a play button that plays exactly that stretch of her recording.
- **The whole thing.** Her full recall with the text off screen, marked against
  the whole answer key, plus "hear it done right" from the recording of the same
  stimulus already stored as the standard.
- **A short verdict** on how close the whole question came to the standard, with
  the big misses named rather than a wall of detail.

Section header stays as it is: overall band, coverage, time, pattern note.

## How the line split works

No extra key presses and no change to the screen — the split is worked out after
the fact from the one recording:

1. The recording is transcribed with word-level times (this already happens).
2. The words spoken while the text was on screen are matched to the lines of that
   stimulus, in order, so each line gets the stretch of speech that belongs to it.
   Order is fixed (she works top to bottom) and every line's own text is known, so
   the match is anchored, not a guess.
3. A line she never spoke about is marked as skipped rather than being credited.
4. The words spoken after she pressed Space are the whole-thing recall, unchanged.

Each line then carries its own start/end offsets into the single recording, which
is what makes per-line playback possible without ever splitting the file.

## Grading

- One AI marking pass per question, covering all of its lines plus the full recall
  in a single call, against the authored key points and the stored standard
  recording for that stimulus. Judgement is "close enough in her own words", not
  word matching, and reciting the line back verbatim still counts as a miss.
- Marking runs the moment she submits the finished section, not on a timer. The
  Report Card opens on a "marking your section" state and fills in as soon as the
  pass returns. The existing nightly sweeper stays as a backstop for a closed tab.

## Answer key check

Before building, an audit of every section in the library: every stimulus has
lines, every line has key points, and a standard recording exists for it. Anything
missing is listed for you rather than silently graded as zero.

## Technical notes

- `useSectionRecorder` / `SectionRunner` unchanged apart from continuing to stamp
  the existing `working` / `recalling` boundaries per stimulus.
- New alignment step in `grade-rrc-section`: cut the Deepgram word stream by
  `rrc.audio_segments`, then attribute the `working` words to `chunks[]` via the
  judge call; write `lines[]` into the existing `activity_section_grades.stimuli`
  jsonb (no schema change).
- Judge contract in `_shared/rrcGrading.ts` extended: input gains the chunk texts
  and the `model_runs` transcript for the stimulus; output gains per-chunk
  `{ n, spoken, start_ms, end_ms, details_caught[], details_missed[], verdict }`
  and a whole-stimulus closeness note.
- `SectionReportCard.tsx` gains a per-line block reusing the existing `useClip`
  seek-in-one-file player; ADR 0020 (one recording, many clips) still holds.
- New ADR recording that line attribution is derived after the run and never
  tracked live, so ADR 0013 (she chunks freely) is not contradicted.
- Client invokes grading immediately after the upload resolves and the Report Card
  polls; sweeper unto...

> **Additional context needed**: what the interface is trying to accomplish.

### Gather Assessments

Launch two independent assessments. **Neither may see the other's output** — this isolation is what makes the combined score honest. Running both in one head silently anchors them to each other; do not shortcut it for cost, speed, or context-size reasons.

Delegate each assessment to a separate sub-agent (Claude Code's `Agent` tool, Codex's subagent spawning, etc.). Each returns structured findings as text. Do NOT output findings to the user yet.

Fall back to sequential in-head work only if the environment genuinely cannot spawn sub-agents.

**Tab isolation**: When browser automation is available, each assessment MUST create its own new tab. Never reuse an existing tab, even if one is already open at the correct URL. This prevents the two assessments from interfering with each other's page state.

#### Assessment A: LLM Design Review

Read the relevant source files (HTML, CSS, JS/TS) and, if browser automation is available, visually inspect the live page. **Create a new tab** for this; do not reuse existing tabs. After navigation, label the tab by setting the document title:
```javascript
document.title = '[LLM] ' + document.title;
```
Think like a design director. Evaluate:

**AI Slop Detection (CRITICAL)**: Does this look like every other AI-generated interface? Review against ALL **DON'T** guidelines from the parent impeccable skill (already loaded in this context). Check for AI color palette, gradient text, dark glows, glassmorphism, hero metric layouts, identical card grids, generic fonts, and all other tells. **The test**: If someone said "AI made this," would you believe them immediately?

**Holistic Design Review**: visual hierarchy (eye flow, primary action clarity), information architecture (structure, grouping, cognitive load), emotional resonance (does it match brand and audience?), discoverability (are interactive elements obvious?), composition (balance, whitespace, rhythm), typography (hierarchy, readability, font choices), color (purposeful use, cohesion, accessibility), states & edge cases (empty, loading, error, success), microcopy (clarity, tone, helpfulness).

**Cognitive Load** (consult [cognitive-load](cognitive-load.md)):
- Run the 8-item cognitive load checklist. Report failure count: 0-1 = low (good), 2-3 = moderate, 4+ = critical.
- Count visible options at each decision point. If >4, flag it.
- Check for progressive disclosure: is complexity revealed only when needed?

**Emotional Journey**:
- What emotion does this interface evoke? Is that intentional?
- **Peak-end rule**: Is the most intense moment positive? Does the experience end well?
- **Emotional valleys**: Check for anxiety spikes at high-stakes moments (payment, delete, commit). Are there design interventions (progress indicators, reassurance copy, undo options)?

**Nielsen's Heuristics** (consult [heuristics-scoring](heuristics-scoring.md)):
Score each of the 10 heuristics 0-4. This scoring will be presented in the report.

Return structured findings covering: AI slop verdict, heuristic scores, cognitive load assessment, what's working (2-3 items), priority issues (3-5 with what/why/fix), minor observations, and provocative questions.

#### Assessment B: Automated Detection

Run the bundled deterministic detector, which flags 25 specific patterns (AI slop tells + general design quality).

**CLI scan**:
```bash
npx impeccable --json [--fast] [target]
```

- Pass HTML/JSX/TSX/Vue/Svelte files or directories as `[target]` (anything with markup). Do not pass CSS-only files.
- For URLs, skip the CLI scan (it requires Puppeteer). Use browser visualization instead.
- For large directories (200+ scannable files), use `--fast` (regex-only, skips jsdom)
- For 500+ files, narrow scope or ask the user
- Exit code 0 = clean, 2 = findings

# Activity Centre — surface brief

## Scope and mode

The Activity Centre wing: the home list (`/activities`), each Activity's intro, and the eight worlds that fill the runner's left column. Mode: **Experience** for the worlds (the student is inside the room the skill lives in), **Operate** for the answer loop on the right, which stays on the module's cream surface untouched.

## Direction (locked 2026-08-21)

**Eight worlds, eight universes.** Every Activity opens into a fully committed physical scene, saturated and material, scoped behind its wrapper class so nothing leaks into the shell. This is a deliberate, owner-approved departure from the app-wide monochrome-editorial restraint: the Centre is the one wing where the room IS the teaching instrument. The Coach and answer loop remain the constant; the room changes.

| World | Activity | Scene | Display face | Key hues |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Polarity Chamber | Negation Station | obsidian void split by an aurora; survey-grid floor; prismatic glass plate | Italiana | ultraviolet 275°, polar cyan 190° |
| Specimen Lab | Vocab Rehab | Victorian naturalist's study; green wall, parchment bench, bell jar on brass plinth | EB Garamond | botanical green 162°, brass 40°, madder 8° |
| Signal Observatory | Spot the Relationship | celestial cartographer's dome; nebulae, twinkling stars, gilt ribs | Marcellus | indigo 233°, gold 45°, comet cyan 187°, rose 330° |
| Switchyard | Conditional Rephrase | midnight rail yard; sodium floods, fog, converging track fan, solari board | Barlow Condensed | steel-night 213°, sodium 36°, signal emerald 158° |
| Argument Atelier | Find the Conclusion | stained-glass workshop; lamp cone, dust, tinted panes in lead cames | Young Serif | umber 24°, lamp gold 40°, glass tints rotating |
| Debate Chamber | Rebuttal Rephrase | torchlit porphyry rotunda; bronze protractor inlaid in the floor | Cinzel | porphyry 356°, torchlight 36°, bronze 30° |
| The Press | Dumb it Down | hot-metal print shop; ink rollers, iron frame, vermilion proof red | Bevan | iron 20°, paper cream 42°, vermilion 14° |
| The Loom | Spot the Callback | indigo dye-house; warp threads, linen sheets with selvage, saffron thread | Alegreya | indigo 236°, linen 43°, saffron 42° |

Statement/body text across dark worlds runs Spectral 300; micro-labels stay on the shared `--ac-mono`.

## Standing rules

- **Scene layers are furniture, not content.** Every world's atmosphere lives in one `aria-hidden` layer stack (`.po-room`, `.sl-room`, …) painted below `z-index: 2` content; tested DOM never gains or loses nodes for scenery.
- **Meaning colors survive grayscale.** Hue is atmosphere on top of geometry/weight/position, never the only carrier of a verdict. Emerald still means "earned/true" wherever it appears.
- **One orchestrated entrance per world**, durations pinned by tests (1800–2500ms), always skippable, `useReducedMotion` short-circuits to `onDone()`.
- **Reduced motion**: every world CSS ends with a `prefers-reduced-motion` block; continuous loops stop rather than speed up.
- **The home is a hall of doorways.** Each `ActivityCard` carries a pure-CSS diorama of its world (`activity-scenes.css`, keyed by slug); the card's facts stay on the module paper below the scene. The intro borrows `--act-accent` from its world via the registry.
- **Registry accents** (`worlds/registry.tsx`) are each world's signature hue and feed the card eyebrow + intro accent.

## Unresolved

- Negation Station is not in `SEED_ACTIVITIES`, so its diorama only shows once assigned.
- Progress ornaments (chips, plaques, mileposts, seals…) remain unreachable from the runner; feeding them real verdicts is its own piece of work.

# Cognitive Load Assessment

Cognitive load is the total mental effort required to use an interface. Overloaded users make mistakes, get frustrated, and leave. This reference helps identify and fix cognitive overload.

---

## Three Types of Cognitive Load

### Intrinsic Load — The Task Itself
Complexity inherent to what the user is trying to do. You can't eliminate this, but you can structure it.

**Manage it by**:
- Breaking complex tasks into discrete steps
- Providing scaffolding (templates, defaults, examples)
- Progressive disclosure — show what's needed now, hide the rest
- Grouping related decisions together

### Extraneous Load — Bad Design
Mental effort caused by poor design choices. **Eliminate this ruthlessly** — it's pure waste.

**Common sources**:
- Confusing navigation that requires mental mapping
- Unclear labels that force users to guess meaning
- Visual clutter competing for attention
- Inconsistent patterns that prevent learning
- Unnecessary steps between user intent and result

### Germane Load — Learning Effort
Mental effort spent building understanding. This is *good* cognitive load — it leads to mastery.

**Support it by**:
- Progressive disclosure that reveals complexity gradually
- Consistent patterns that reward learning
- Feedback that confirms correct understanding
- Onboarding that teaches through action, not walls of text

---

## Cognitive Load Checklist

Evaluate the interface against these 8 items:

- [ ] **Single focus**: Can the user complete their primary task without distraction from competing elements?
- [ ] **Chunking**: Is information presented in digestible groups (≤4 items per group)?
- [ ] **Grouping**: Are related items visually grouped together (proximity, borders, shared background)?
- [ ] **Visual hierarchy**: Is it immediately clear what's most important on the screen?
- [ ] **One thing at a time**: Can the user focus on a single decision before moving to the next?
- [ ] **Minimal choices**: Are decisions simplified (≤4 visible options at any decision point)?
- [ ] **Working memory**: Does the user need to remember information from a previous screen to act on the current one?
- [ ] **Progressive disclosure**: Is complexity revealed only when the user needs it?

**Scoring**: Count the failed items. 0–1 failures = low cognitive load (good). 2–3 = moderate (address soon). 4+ = high cognitive load (critical fix needed).

---

## The Working Memory Rule

**Humans can hold ≤4 items in working memory at once** (Miller's Law revised by Cowan, 2001).

At any decision point, count the number of distinct options, actions, or pieces of information a user must simultaneously consider:
- **≤4 items**: Within working memory limits — manageable
- **5–7 items**: Pushing the boundary — consider grouping or progressive disclosure
- **8+ items**: Overloaded — users will skip, misclick, or abandon

**Practical applications**:
- Navigation menus: ≤5 top-level items (group the rest under clear categories)
- Form sections: ≤4 fields visible per group before a visual break
- Action buttons: 1 primary, 1–2 secondary, group the rest in a menu
- Dashboard widgets: ≤4 key metrics visible without scrolling
- Pricing tiers: ≤3 options (more causes analysis paralysis)

---

## Common Cognitive Load Violations

### 1. The Wall of Options
**Problem**: Presenting 10+ choices at once with no hierarchy.
**Fix**: Group into categories, highlight recommended, use progressive disclosure.

### 2. The Memory Bridge
**Problem**: User must remember info from step 1 to complete step 3.
**Fix**: Keep relevant context visible, or repeat it where it's needed.

### 3. The Hidden Navigation
**Problem**: User must build a mental map of where things are.
**Fix**: Always show current location (breadcrumbs, active states, progress indicators).

### 4. The Jargon Barrier
**Problem**: Technical or domain language forces translation effort.

import { createClient } from "npm:@supabase/supabase-js@2";

/**
 * Re-drive Read, Recall, Colour sections that finished but never got a grade.
 *
 * The client asks for grading the moment a section ends, and does not wait on
 * the answer. That ask is an optimisation and it is lost easily: the tab closed
 * before the request left, the network dropped, the function was cold, the
 * judge was down. This is the guarantee behind it -- the thing that means a
 * closed tab never costs a Report Card, as
 * `docs/specs/2026-09-01-rrc-section-grader.md` §4 promised and nothing
 * delivered.
 *
 * It re-drives a run only once that run is FINISHED. Stimuli are written as
 * they happen now, so a half-done section has rows too, and rows are no longer
 * evidence that anything is over. A section is the unit of work and half a
 * section is not a partial score, so the finished mark is the gate. Saving and
 * judging are different acts.
 *
 * It re-drives two states, and only after the run has had ten minutes to grade
 * itself:
 *
 * - a finished section with no grade row at all, and
 * - a grade row the grader itself marked incomplete, because some stimuli went
 *   unjudged and it wrote what it had rather than throwing the run away.
 *
 * It holds no lease and takes no lock. It does not need one: `grade-rrc-section`
 * returns an existing complete grade untouched, so two sweeps that overlap cost
 * one wasted request rather than two grades.
 *
 * NOT SCHEDULED, and that is a decision rather than an oversight (2026-09-04).
 *
 * This comment used to say it was "scheduled externally, on the pattern in
 * `docs/ops/native-single-session-workers.md`". It never was: `cron.job` holds
 * four jobs and all four are booking and calendar. Joshua's call, once that was
 * shown, is to leave it unscheduled -- a section grades itself on submit, so
 * this is the backstop and not the path, and scheduling it would put a worker
 * secret inline in a cron command the way the booking jobs' already is.
 *
 * So nothing runs this today. It is invoked by hand, and everything below still
 * holds for when it is. What stays uncovered is a run whose client-side ask
 * never left the browser: it has the 120-second retry and nothing else. See
 * `docs/adr/0021-a-partial-grade-is-written-and-says-so.md`.
 */

const WORKER_NAME = "sweep-rrc-grading";

/** A run grades itself first. Only after this is it ours to re-drive. */
const GRACE_MINUTES = 10;

/**
 * How many sections one sweep will grade.
 *
 * Each is a transcription plus a full section's judging, and they run one after
 * another so that a backlog cannot put this function past its own wall clock --
 * which is the exact failure it exists to repair. Three a run, every ten
 * minutes, drains far faster than one tutor's students can fill it.
 */
const MAX_PER_RUN = 3;

/** How many section assignments to consider. RRC sections only, so it is small. */
const SCAN_LIMIT = 500;

function json(body: unknown, status = 200) {
  return new Response(JSON.stringify(body), {
    status,
    headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json", "Cache-Control": "no-store" },
  });
}

function requiredEnv(name: string): string {
  const value = Deno.env.get(name);
  if (!value) throw new Error(`${name.toLowerCase()}_not_configured`);
  return value;
}

/** Constant-time, so the secret is not probed a byte at a time. */
function authorize(req: Request): boolean {
  const expected = requiredEnv("BOOKING_WORKER_SECRET");
  const actual = req.headers.get("x-worker-secret") ?? "";

# 0021 — A partial grade is written, and says so

Date: 2026-09-03
Status: accepted
Extends [0019](./0019-a-grade-the-student-can-write-is-not-a-grade.md).

## Context

The first end-to-end trial of a Read, Recall, Colour section ran on 2026-09-03:
PT102.S3, 22 stimuli, 21m34s against a 25-minute goal, driven through the real
product. Everything up to the grade worked. The recording was one unbroken
21.5-minute file, the upload landed, the clock proof held to within 1 ms, and all
22 response rows were written with their phase boundaries.

`grade-rrc-section` then returned 504, and `activity_section_grades` was empty --
not for this run, for its whole life. The production grader had never
successfully written a row.

The cause is in the shape of the function rather than in any of its logic. It
judged the 22 stimuli in a `for` loop, one model call after another, and 22 round
trips do not fit inside an edge function's wall clock. Judged all at once against
the same saved transcript, the same calls take about 20 seconds.

Two further things follow from that loop, and both are worse than the timeout.
A single failed judge call threw out of the loop, past the write, and lost the
whole section -- including the transcription already paid for. And because the
client `await`ed a request it documents as fire-and-forget, the student sat on
"Section finished" for the entire 150 seconds before the 504, with a Report Card
screen already built and polling on the other side of it.

## Decision

**The stimuli are judged concurrently, at a fixed bound.** Eight at a time,
through one helper in the shared grading module. Bounded rather than unbounded
because the judge is a rate-limited API and an unbounded fan-out trades a timeout
for a wall of 429s. Results come back in section order, not completion order,
because the Report Card is read top to bottom.

**A stimulus that cannot be judged costs its own grade and not the section's.**
The failure is caught inside the per-stimulus work, and that stimulus is returned
carrying `ungraded` with the reason. The section is written with whatever landed.
A run that was recorded, uploaded and transcribed correctly is never discarded
because a model call failed.

**`ungraded` is not zero, and every count knows the difference.** An unjudged
stimulus is excluded from Coverage's denominator and from the Reciting, Listing,
uncoloured and decoy tallies. Counted as a zero it would deflate her Coverage;
counted as uncoloured or as a decoy pass it would report our outage as her habit.
On the trial data, two failed judge calls would have shown 82% instead of 91%,
and six uncoloured stimuli instead of four.

**`section.complete` says whether every stimulus actually got a judgement**, and
it is the one flag the sweeper reads. A partial grade must not pass for a
finished one, or the run that most needs re-driving is the one nothing will ever
look at again. A grade row written before this flag existed has no `complete` key
and is treated as complete: it is a real grade, and re-driving every one of them
would be a re-grade, not a sweep.

**A stored grade keeps the section's authored shape.** Every stimulus grade
carries its `item_order`, and the section summary carries `stimuli_in_section`

import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";

const REPO = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "..");

function readEnv(file: string): Record<string, string> {
  const out: Record<string, string> = {};
  if (!fs.existsSync(file)) return out;
  for (const line of fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8").split(/\r?\n/)) {
    const t = line.trim();
    if (!t || t.startsWith("#")) continue;
    const i = t.indexOf("=");
    if (i < 0) continue;
    out[t.slice(0, i).trim()] = t.slice(i + 1).trim().replace(/^["']|["']$/g, "");
  }
  return out;
}
const env = {
  ...readEnv(path.resolve(REPO, "../../.env")),
  ...readEnv(path.join(REPO, ".env")),
};

function arg(n: string) {
  const i = process.argv.indexOf(`--${n}`);
  return i >= 0 ? process.argv[i + 1] : undefined;
}
const query = arg("student");
const days = Number(arg("days") ?? 30);

/**
 * Ask the server to grade a finished section.
 *
 * Fire and forget, deliberately. The grade is produced by an edge function that
 * transcribes the recording and judges every stimulus, which takes far longer
 * than the student should be made to wait on a promise, and a closed tab must

 * guarantee. A failure here is logged and swallowed for the same reason.
 */
export async function requestSectionGrading(assignmentId: string): Promise<void> {

  } catch (err) {

  }
}

 * could set herself (ADR 0019).

async function transcribe(audio: ArrayBuffer, contentType: string): Promise<Word[]> {
  const key = Deno.env.get("DEEPGRAM_API_KEY");
  if (!key) throw new Error("DEEPGRAM_API_KEY is not configured");
  const res = await fetch(
    "https://api.deepgram.com/v1/listen?model=nova-3&punctuate=true&smart_format=true",
    {
      method: "POST",
      headers: { Authorization: [REDACTED] ${key}`, "Content-Type": contentType },
      body: audio,
    },
  );
  const body = await res.json();
  if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`Deepgram ${res.status}: ${JSON.stringify(body).slice(0, 300)}`);
  const words = body?.results?.channels?.[0]?.alternatives?.[0]?.words ?? [];
  return words.map((w: Record<string, unknown>) => ({
    word: String(w.punctuated_word ?? w.word ?? ""),
    start: Number(w.start ?? 0),
    end: Number(w.end ?? 0),
  }));
}

/**
 * The stretch of the recording a set of windows covers, in milliseconds.
 *
 * One span rather than the list of windows, because what it is for is a seek:
 * play from here to there. Null when there is nothing to play.
 */
function span(segments: Segment[]): { start: number; end: number } | undefined {
  if (!segments.length) return undefined;
  return {
    start: Math.min(...segments.map((s) => s.startMs)),
    end: Math.max(...segments.map((s) => s.endMs)),
  };
}

/** The words spoken inside any of this stimulus's windows, in order. */
function wordsIn(words: Word[], segments: Segment[]): Word[] {
  return words.filter((w) =>
    segments.some((s) => w.start * 1000 >= s.startMs && w.start * 1000 < s.endMs),
  );
}

/**
 * The longest silence inside The Recall.
 *
 * Windowed to `recalling` on purpose. She reads silently, so a gap during
 * `working` is a read and is correct; an unwindowed detector fires on every
 * stimulus and is worse than none.
 */

  let worst = 0;

    const inside = words.filter((w) => w.start * 1000 >= seg.startMs && w.start * 1000 < seg.endMs);
    let cursor = seg.startMs / 1000;

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Patch the grader to transcribe by signed URL and type-check it if Deno exists
command: node - <<'EOF'
const fs = require("fs");
const p = "supabase/functions/grade-rrc-section/index.ts";
let s = fs.readFileSync(p, "utf8");
const before = s;

// 1. transcribe takes a URL, not bytes.
s = s.replace(
`async function transcribe(audio: ArrayBuffer, contentType: string): Promise<Word[]> {
  const key = Deno.env.get("DEEPGRAM_API_KEY");
  if (!key) throw new Error("DEEPGRAM_API_KEY is not configured");
  const res = await fetch(
    "https://api.deepgram.com/v1/listen?model=nova-3&punctuate=true&smart_format=true",
    {
      method: "POST",
      headers: { Authorization: [REDACTED] \${key}\`, "Content-Type": contentType },
      body: audio,
    },
  );`,
`/**
 * Transcribe the recording Deepgram can fetch at \`audioUrl\`.
 *
 * By URL, never by bytes. This function used to download the recording and
 * post the buffer, which held the whole file in an edge function's memory
 * twice over. A 20-minute section fit; a 66-minute one (112 MB, Celia,
 * 2026-09-08) did not, and the function died with WORKER_RESOURCE_LIMIT before
 * a single word was transcribed -- the one failure the sweeper cannot repair
 * by re-driving, because every re-drive dies the same way. Deepgram fetches
 * the file itself from a short-lived signed URL, so this function never holds
 * the audio and the length of a section stops being a limit on grading it.
 */
async function transcribe(audioUrl: string): Promise<Word[]> {
  const key = Deno.env.get("DEEPGRAM_API_KEY");
  if (!key) throw new Error("DEEPGRAM_API_KEY is not configured");
  const res = await fetch(
    "https://api.deepgram.com/v1/listen?model=nova-3&punctuate=true&smart_format=true",
    {
      method: "POST",
      headers: { Authorization: [REDACTED] \${key}\`, "Content-Type": "application/json" },
      body: JSON.stringify({ url: audioUrl }),
    },
  );`);

// 2. Sign a URL instead of downloading.
s = s.replace(
`    const { data: file, error: dErr } = await admin.storage
      .from((first.audio_bucket as string) ?? "drill-recordings")
      .download(audioPath);
    if (dErr) throw dErr;

    const bytes = await file.arrayBuffer();
    const words = await transcribe(bytes, audioPath.endsWith(".m4a") ? "audio/mp4" : "audio/webm");`,
`    /*
     * A signed read, good for long enough to transcribe the longest section
     * anyone has recorded and no longer. The bucket is private; the URL is the
     * only thing Deepgram is handed, and it is never stored.
     */
    const { data: signed, error: sErr } = await admin.storage
      .from((first.audio_bucket as string) ?? "drill-recordings")
      .createSignedUrl(audioPath, 15 * 60);
    if (sErr) throw sErr;
    if (!signed?.signedUrl) throw new Error("could not sign a URL for the recording");

    const words = await transcribe(signed.signedUrl);`);

if (s === before) { console.error("NO CHANGE MADE"); process.exit(1); }
fs.writeFileSync(p, s);
console.log("patched");
EOF

[/external_agent_tool_call]

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Pull, commit the grader fix, and push to main
command: git pull -q --ff-only origin main && git add supabase/functions/grade-rrc-section/index.ts && git -c core.safecrlf=false commit -q -F - <<'EOF'
Transcribe RRC recordings by signed URL, not by buffered bytes

grade-rrc-section downloaded the whole recording into the edge function
and posted the buffer to Deepgram. A 20-minute section fit in memory; a
66-minute one (112 MB, 2026-09-08) killed the function with
WORKER_RESOURCE_LIMIT before a single word was transcribed, and every
re-drive died the same way, so the sweeper could not repair it either.

Deepgram now fetches the file itself from a 15-minute signed URL. The
function never holds the audio, and the length of a section is no longer
a limit on grading it.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF
git log --oneline -3 && git push -q origin main && echo PUSHED && git rev-parse --short HEAD
[/external_agent_tool_call]

description: Write a poll script that re-drives the grader until a grade row appears
command: cat > scripts/_poll-grade.sh <<'EOF'
#!/bin/sh
# Re-drive the grader every 90 s until Celia's grade row lands or 12 minutes pass.
i=0
while [ $i -lt 8 ]; do
  sleep 90
  i=$((i+1))
  out=$(npx vite-node scripts/_grade-celia.ts 2>&1 | grep -v "^\s*at " | grep -E "status|error|body|grade rows now|returned in" | tr '\n' ' ')
  echo "poll $i: $out"
  echo "$out" | grep -q "grade rows now: \[{" && exit 0
done
echo "TIMEOUT: no grade row after 12 min"
exit 1
EOF
echo written
[/external_agent_tool_call]

Everything marked "live" was measured in the browser; everything marked "source" is from the files.

# Assessment A: LLM design review of the RRC section briefing page

**Visual inspection:** happened at desktop (viewport reported 2048 CSS px wide at DPR 0.94, page column 840px). Reached via `/activities` -&gt; "Choose a section" -&gt; library -&gt; "Start" on PT21.S3 (the library Start opens the briefing, not the mic; I did not press "Start the section"). The rendered route is `/activities/3982be97-6957-4223-8350-c607218199da`, `data-runner="section-briefing"`. Mobile (390px) was attempted twice; `resize_window` returned success but `innerWidth` stayed 2048 and the capture then broke, so **mobile is reasoned from the CSS, not seen.**

Note: live content matched the repo JSON (`read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json`) for every heading and step I checked, so the copy findings apply to what students see.

## 1. AI slop verdict

**Register: PRODUCT.** Would a user fluent in Linear/Notion trust it? Mostly yes. It is a plain, single-column, four-card procedure with one obvious primary action, and that is the right shape for a pre-recording briefing. It does not look machine-made: Fraunces title, cream ground, tight microcopy, no hero metric, no icon-grid.

Reflex checks:
- Side-stripe borders: none. All borders 1px (`section-launch.css:1887`, `:1909`).
- Gradient text: none.
- Glassmorphism: the door's `.sl-chip` has `backdrop-filter: blur(8px)` (`:224`), but the briefing override sets it to `none` (`:1796`). Clean.
- Hero-metric template: none.
- Identical card grid: the four step cards are identical boxes, but a numbered procedure in identical boxes is the correct reading, not the pattern being banned.
- Modal-as-first-thought: none. It is a page. Good.
- Em dashes: **none in the page** (live DOM scan of `.sl` found zero U+2014/U+2013). Out of scope but observed: the RRC card on `/activities` reads "start it now — every attempt is kept" with an em dash. Different surface; passing it up.
- `#000`/`#fff` literals: `--rd-on-dark: #fff` (`:19`, sanctioned). No other hex literals in the briefing rules. `rgba(255,255,255,...)` appears in `.sl-start`'s box-shadow ring (`:275-277`, `:290-291`) and `.sl-brief` colour (`:315-317`) as rgba literals rather than tokens. Not a hex literal, but they are white-on-dark values written for the hero that the briefing page inherits and then patches over.
- Pure untinted neutrals: none. Ink is indigo-tinted (`#16132b`), paper is warm.

Where it does drift toward generic: the "Start the section" button is a landing-page pill (52px, `border-radius: 9999px`, drop shadow, `translateY(-2px)` lift on hover, `:262-295`). On the dark hero that is the door's persuade register. On this cream task page it reads as a marketing CTA pasted onto a checklist, and the Product register bans lift and decorative motion.

## 2. Heuristic scores

| # | Heuristic | Score | Key issue |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Visibility of system status | 2 | The notice says nothing is recording, which is good. But the page never states the one thing about status that matters next: that the run is one take with no pause and about 25 minutes. Verified live: with the long version open, "one take" appears nowhere on the page, and "pause" appears once, in an unrelated sense. |
| 2 | Match with real world | 3 | Plain language throughout; "The Recall." as a step label is a proper noun that a first-timer reads as a typo for "Recall it." |
| 3 | User control and freedom | 3 | "Back" exists and is 44x44 (live). No confirmation before Start, which is right for a non-destructive action. |
| 4 | Consistency and standards | 2 | Three different micro-label styles on one page: mono chip (`:211`), Inter uppercase h2 (`:1872`), Inter uppercase step number (`:1892`), while the file itself declares `.sl-label` as "the one micro-label style" (`:71`). Two `&lt;h2&gt;`s render at different weights (one uppercase 15px/600, one 16px/400 inside `&lt;summary&gt;`, `:1841`). Focus ring is ink on buttons and ink-2 on the summary (`:1859`). |
| 5 | Error prevention | 3 | Nothing records until Start, and Start is unambiguous. Missing: no browser-permission warning, so the first press on a fresh browser opens a permission dialog she was not told about. |
| 6 | Recognition rather than recall | 3 | Steps are all open, numbered, labelled. The "+" text glyph as the only disclosure affordance (`:1852`) is weak. |
| 7 | Flexibility and efficiency | 2 | Tab order is Start, Back, summary, Start: two presses to the primary. No accelerator; the runner uses Space as "the only key" but Space/Enter on the page does nothing until you focus the button. Reasonable for a briefing. |
| 8 | Aesthetic and minimalist | 3 | Chip and H1 both say "Read, Recall, Colour"; the chip is decorative repetition on this page. Foot sentence repeats the notice's fact. Otherwise tight. |
| 9 | Error recovery | 2 | No state on this page can error, so no messaging exists. The mic-denied case is handled downstream, not here, and there is no hint of it. Scored on what a nervous student meets if she declines the permission. |
| 10 | Help and documentation | 4 | The long version is exactly the right contextual help: one click, in place, task-organised. |

**Total: 27/40 (Acceptable, upper end).** The foundations are right; the misses are content gaps and vocabulary drift, not structure.

## 3. Cognitive load: 1 failure (low)

- Single focus: pass.
- Chunking: pass (4 steps).
- Grouping: pass, with a caveat below.
- Visual hierarchy: pass.
- One thing at a time: pass.
- Minimal choices: pass (Start, Back, expand).
- Working memory: **fail.** She has to carry "one take, no pause, ~25 minutes" from the library page (or from nowhere) into the moment of pressing Start; this page does not restate it.
- Progressive disclosure: pass. Collapsed long version is the correct move.

## 4. Emotional journey

**The peak** is the notice: "Nothing is recording yet. The microphone opens when you press Start." That single sentence is the best thing on the page. It is rendered as a 15px ink-2 line in a box styled exactly like the four step cards beneath it (same paper fill, same 1px line, radius 16 vs 16, `:720-729` vs `:1887-1890`). The one sentence written to lower her shoulders is visually a fifth card in the procedure.

**The end** (pressing Start): the foot says "When you are ready, press Start. The clock begins counting up and the recording begins." That is the moment of highest anxiety and the page answers "what happens now" but not "how long am I committing to" or "can I stop". The code comment at `SectionBriefingPage.tsx:63-66` says the one-take fact "is said in Step 4 and again in the full instructions." It is not in Step 4 (`instructions.json` step 4 body: "Press space. The stimulus disappears..."), and it is not in the full instructions either. It lives only in `instructions.rules[0]`, which this page does not render (`SectionBriefingPage.tsx` never touches `instructions.rules`). The three-fact notice was cut to one fact on the assumption the other two survived elsewhere; they did not.

Also lost: `briefing.closer` ("One a day holds you steady. The second one is where you improve.") is typed as "The last beat before the Start action" (`types.ts:243`) and is never rendered on this page. The authored end-note for the peak-end moment is dropped.

## 5. What's working

1. **The decision to make it a page, not a modal, and to put the four steps open and numbered.** Every step is a label plus one short paragraph; the copy is the best on the platform ("Laughing is fine." "It does not have to be clever, it has to be yours.").
2. **The hero classes are honestly re-grounded for paper** (`:1763-1834`): title 46px ink, tagline without text-shadow, chip without blur, Back visible. Someone measured this in a browser and fixed what was invisible, and the comments say why.
3. **Accessibility basics hold, verified live:** heading outline H1 -&gt; H2 -&gt; H2 -&gt; H3, Back is 44x44, focus rings render on Back and the summary at 2px, `aria-label` on the steps section, no live-region noise on a static sentence.

## 6. Priority issues

**P1. The two facts she most needs before Start are not on the page.**
What: "one take, no pause" and "about 25 minutes" appear nowhere visible. Verified live with the long version open: zero hits for "one take"; "minute" only inside the collapsed essay's sixth heading.
Why: this is the anxiety moment. She presses Start not knowing she cannot pause for 25 minutes. The corrections log removed both from the notice and the meta line on the belief they survived in Step 4; `SectionBriefingPage.tsx:63-66` documents that belief and it is false.
Fix: put one plain sentence in the foot, next to the foot Start, where it matters: "It runs in one take, about twenty-five minutes, and it does not pause." Or render `briefing.closer` there as the type intended. Update the code comment.

**P1. The notice is styled as a fifth step card.**
What: `.sl-notice` (`:720-729`) and `.sl-bp-list li` (`:1887-1890`) share fill, hairline, and radius. Live: notice at y=341, first card at y=469, identical treatment.
Why: the one reassuring sentence is visually demoted to procedure.
Fix: take the box off it. Set it as a plain line under the CTA in ink, 16px, no border, so it reads as the page speaking rather than a step to complete.

**P2. Step body line length is ~100+ characters.**
What: `.sl-bp-list li p` renders at 798px wide, 16px Inter (live). Three lines of Step 1 run roughly 105 characters each. `.sl-essay` sets `max-width: 68ch` (`:1155`) but the step list has no measure.
Why: product prose should hold 65-75ch; these are the sentences she must actually follow.
Fix: `max-width: 62ch` on `.sl-bp-list li p` and `.sl-notice`, or narrow `.sl-briefing-page` from 880 to ~720px.

**P2. Micro-label vocabulary is three-way inconsistent, and the two H2s do not match.**
What: chip in Plex Mono 11px (`:211`), section h2 in Inter uppercase 15px/600 (`:1872`), step number in Inter uppercase 12px at opacity .7 (`:1892`). `.sl-label` at `:71` is the declared single style and is unused here. The `&lt;summary&gt;` h2 inherits body font (`:1841`) so "Read the long version +" is 16px/400 while its sibling h2 is uppercase 600.
Why: same rank, three looks; the collapsed heading looks like a stray sentence, which hurts discoverability of the only help on the page.
Fix: put `.sl-bp-n` and `.sl-bp-h2` on `.sl-label` (mono), and give the summary the same h2 treatment as "What you do" with a real chevron.

**P2. The lede is silently overridden.**
What: `.rd-lede` sets 20px Fraunces italic (`:1157-1163`), but `.sl-essay p` (`:1161`, specificity 0,1,1) beats it (0,1,0). Live: lede renders at 16.5px.
Why: the essay's opening line is meant to lead and instead sits at body size.
Fix: `.sl-essay p.rd-lede` or move the size onto the `.sl-essay .rd-lede` selector.

**P3. Landing-page button on a task page.**
What: `.sl-start` lift and shadow on hover (`:286-295`), rgba white ring (`:275`) written for the dark hero.
Why: Product register bans lift; DESIGN.md's button character is "does not lift." The surface brief sanctions the world's tokens, not the persuade register's motion inside its Operate slab.
Fix: for `.sl-briefing-page .sl-start`, drop the transform and the outer shadow; keep the fill darkening.

## 7. Minor observations

- Chip and H1 both say "Read, Recall, Colour"; the H1 also carries "· PT21 Section 3". The chip is redundant here (it earned its place on the hero where the H1 is the bare name).
- `.sl-bp-head` is `justify-content: space-between` (`:1761`) intending CTA at right, but at 840px the title block (613px) plus CTA (250px) plus gap does not fit, so the CTA always wraps under the tagline. The layout that ships is accidental, not designed. Either commit to stacked or narrow the title block.
- Empty states (source): with no steps the h2 and list vanish and the page is head, notice, foot; the foot still says "press Start" with no method shown. With no briefing the details vanish (fine). With no tagline and no subtitle, an empty `&lt;p class="sl-tagline"&gt;` still renders with 12px top margin (`SectionBriefingPage.tsx:49-51`). Long title: `clamp(30px, 3.4vw, 46px)` with `text-wrap: balance` handles a two-line title; "Read, Recall, Colour · PT105 Section 1" was one line at 613px.
- Foot copy: "The clock begins counting up and the recording begins" repeats "begins" and restates the notice's fact, contrary to the log's "say it once."
- Step label "The Recall." reads as a typo next to the three imperatives ("Read it." "Tell it back." "Colour it."). This is a locked product name, so noting rather than recommending.
- Summary focus ring uses `--rd-ink-2` (`:1859`), buttons use `--rd-ink` (`:1776`). One ring colour per ground.
- `.sl-bp-more &gt; summary::after { content: " +" }` (`:1852`): a text glyph as the disclosure affordance. Some screen readers announce the "+".
- `.sl-bp-meta` (`:1866`) is defined and unused on this page.
- Mobile (from CSS, not seen): `@media (max-width: 640px)` makes `.sl-start` full-width (`:1675`) and stacks the head (`:1916`). Top Start will sit around y=300, which is thumb-reachable; the foot Start is the natural one. Card padding 18/20 on a 350px column is fine. No overflow risk found in the rules.

## 8. Persona red flags

**Jordan (first-timer):** reads "The Recall." and hesitates; reads "Press space" in Step 4 and looks for a Space button on this page; presses Start and gets a browser microphone permission dialog with no warning that it is coming. Nothing says "your browser will ask to use the microphone; allow it."

**Sam (screen reader / keyboard):** Tab lands on "Start the section" before any of the content is in the focus path; in browse mode the H1 and notice come first, so this is survivable. `&lt;h2&gt;` nested inside `&lt;summary&gt;` (`SectionBriefingPage.tsx:98-100`): NVDA and VoiceOver expose a summary as a button/disclosure and treat an inline heading inside it inconsistently; some flatten it, so "Read the long version" can drop out of the headings list, which is the very outline this was added to fix. Two H2s at wildly different visual weights are invisible to Sam but the mismatch means sighted-assist users and Sam navigate different structures.

**Casey (mobile, one-handed):** the notice is at the top, not near the foot Start she will actually press after scrolling; by the time her thumb reaches the second button the reassurance is 1,000px above. Duplicate the fact next to the foot button (same fix as P1). Interruption mid-page is harmless (nothing records), which is a genuine strength.

**Project persona, "Priya" (coaching client between sessions, assigned this by Joshua, doing it at 9pm after work):** she wants to know "how long is this and can I stop if my roommate walks in." The page answers neither; the answer is in a collapsed essay under the sixth heading. She also wants to know Joshua will see it ("every attempt is kept" is on the library card, not here). Red flag: the page reads as generic platform instructions; nothing on it is addressed from her coach, and PRODUCT.md's positioning is "a real coach is in it."

## 9. Three provocative questions

1. Why is the one sentence written to calm her styled identically to the four things she has to do?
2. If the runner's "only key" is Space, why does this page make her find and click a pill button, and what would happen if Space started the section from here?
3. The library card already sold her the section. What on this page would be missing if it were nothing but the notice, the four steps, and one Start button at the foot?

result: Assessment A complete for the RRC briefing page: 27/40 on heuristics, cognitive load 1/8 failures, two P1s (the one-take/25-minute facts are absent from the page despite the code comment claiming otherwise; the calming notice is styled as a fifth step card), three P2s, one P3; desktop inspected live, mobile from CSS only.</result>

description: Write the critique report to the vault and check the poller
command: cat > /c/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/wiki/RRC-BRIEFING-CRITIQUE-2026-09-08.md <<'EOF'
# Read, Recall, Colour section briefing page: design critique

Date: 2026-09-08. Method: `$impeccable critique` on
`src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionBriefingPage.tsx`, two isolated
passes (a design review with live desktop inspection, and the deterministic
detector) woven into one report. Related: [[COPY-CORRECTIONS]], [[ASSIGN-AN-ACTIVITY]].

## Design health score

| # | Heuristic | Score | Key issue |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Visibility of system status | 2 | "One take, no pause" and "about 25 minutes" are not on the page |
| 2 | Match system / real world | 3 | "The Recall." reads as a typo beside three imperatives |
| 3 | User control and freedom | 3 | Back is 44x44 and works; nothing else needed |
| 4 | Consistency and standards | 2 | Three micro-label styles; the two H2s render at different weights |
| 5 | Error prevention | 3 | No warning that the browser will ask for the microphone |
| 6 | Recognition rather than recall | 3 | A " +" text glyph is the only disclosure affordance |
| 7 | Flexibility and efficiency | 2 | Space is the runner's only key but does nothing here |
| 8 | Aesthetic and minimalist | 3 | Chip and H1 both say the product name; foot restates the notice |
| 9 | Error recovery | 2 | Mic-denied case has no hint on this page |
| 10 | Help and documentation | 4 | The collapsed long version is exactly right |
| **Total** | | **27/40** | **Acceptable, upper end** |

## Anti-patterns verdict

Does it look AI-made? No. Single column, four numbered steps, one primary
action, Fraunces title on cream, no hero metric, no icon grid, no gradient text,
no side stripes, no em dashes in the rendered DOM. The one drift toward generic
is the Start button: a landing-page pill with a hover lift and drop shadow,
written for the dark hero and inherited by this cream task page.

Deterministic scan: `npx impeccable --json` on the page returned zero findings.
The directory scan of `components/` returned 53 advisory findings, none touching
this page or `section-launch.css` (47 are in `activity-scenes.css`). No false
positives to triage. Browser overlay could not run: impeccable 4.0.4 ships no
`detect.js`, and the page has no CSP, so this is a tooling gap and not a page
problem. Cognitive load: 1 of 8 checks failed (working memory), which is low.

## Overall impression

The structure is right and the copy is the best on the platform. The misses are
content gaps and vocabulary drift, not layout. One of them matters: the page was
edited on 2026-09-07 on the belief that the one-take fact survived in Step 4.
It did not. It lives in `instructions.rules[0]`, which this page never renders.

## What's working

- A page, not a modal, with all four steps open and numbered. "Laughing is
  fine." and "It does not have to be clever, it has to be yours." earn their place.
- The hero classes are honestly re-grounded for paper, and the comments say why.
- Accessibility basics hold, measured live: H1 H2 H2 H3, Back 44x44, 2px focus
  rings, no live-region noise.

## Priority issues

**[P1] The two facts she most needs before Start are not on the page.**
Verified live with the long version open: zero hits for "one take"; "minute"
only inside the collapsed essay's sixth heading. The code comment at
`SectionBriefingPage.tsx:63` claims Step 4 carries it. Step 4 does not.
Fix: one plain sentence beside the foot Start: "It runs in one take, about
twenty-five minutes, and it does not pause." Or render `briefing.closer`,
which the type describes as "the last beat before the Start action" and which
nothing renders. Correct the comment. Command: `$impeccable clarify`.

**[P1] The calming notice is styled as a fifth step card.**
`.sl-notice` and `.sl-bp-list li` share fill, hairline, and radius. The one
sentence written to lower her shoulders reads as procedure.
Fix: take the box off it. A plain ink line under the CTA. Command: `$impeccable layout`.

**[P2] Step body lines run about 105 characters.** The essay caps at 68ch; the
step list has no measure. Fix: `max-width: 62ch` on `.sl-bp-list li p` and
`.sl-notice`. Command: `$impeccable typeset`.

**[P2] Three micro-label styles on one page, and mismatched H2s.** Mono chip,
Inter-uppercase h2, Inter-uppercase step number, while `.sl-label` is declared
the one style and goes unused. The summary's h2 inherits body weight, so the
only help on the page looks like a stray sentence. Fix: put step numbers and
h2s on `.sl-label`; give the summary the same h2 treatment and a real chevron.
Command: `$impeccable typeset`.

**[P2] The lede is silently overridden.** `.sl-essay p` outranks `.rd-lede`, so
the 20px italic lede renders at 16.5px. Fix: `.sl-essay p.rd-lede`.
Command: `$impeccable polish`.

**[P3] Landing-page button on a task page.** Drop the transform and outer
shadow on `.sl-briefing-page .sl-start`; keep the fill darkening.
Command: `$impeccable quieter`.

## Persona red flags

- **Jordan (first-timer):** reads "Press space" in Step 4 and looks for a Space
  button; presses Start and meets a microphone permission dialog with no warning.
- **Sam (screen reader):** an `h2` nested inside `summary` is exposed
  inconsistently; some readers drop it from the headings list, the very outline
  the h2 was added to fix.
- **Casey (mobile):** the reassurance sits at the top and the Start she presses
  is 1,000px below it. Same fix as the first P1. Mobile was reasoned from the
  CSS, not seen: the viewport resize did not take.
- **A coaching client at 9pm:** wants "how long is this and can I stop if my
  roommate walks in." The page answers neither, and nothing on it is addressed
  from her coach.

## Minor observations

- Chip and H1 both say "Read, Recall, Colour"; the chip is redundant here.
- The head's side-by-side layout never fits at 840px, so the CTA always wraps
  under the tagline. What ships is accidental.
- Empty tagline still renders an empty `p` with top margin.
- Foot copy repeats "begins" and restates the notice's fact.
- Summary focus ring is `--rd-ink-2`, buttons are `--rd-ink`.
- `.sl-bp-meta` is defined and unused.

## Questions to consider

1. Why is the one sentence written to calm her styled identically to the four things she has to do?
2. If the runner's only key is Space, what would happen if Space started the section from here?
3. What would be missing if the page were only the notice, the four steps, and one Start at the foot?

## Recommended order

1. `$impeccable clarify`: the one-take and 25-minute sentence at the foot, render `closer`, fix the comment.
2. `$impeccable layout`: un-box the notice.
3. `$impeccable typeset`: 62ch measure, one label style, matching H2s, lede fix.
4. `$impeccable quieter`: the Start button's lift and shadow.
5. `$impeccable polish` last.
EOF

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Add a build marker to the grader, commit, and push
command: node - <<'EOF'
const fs = require("fs");
const p = "supabase/functions/grade-rrc-section/index.ts";
let s = fs.readFileSync(p, "utf8"); const crlf = s.includes("\r\n"); s = s.replace(/\r\n/g, "\n");
const before = s;
s = s.replace(
`function json(body: unknown, status = 200) {
  return new Response(JSON.stringify(body), {
    status,
    headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json", "Cache-Control": "no-store" },
  });
}`,
`/**
 * Which build of this function answered. Lovable deploys edge functions on
 * push with no visible receipt, and on 2026-09-08 a fix could not be told
 * apart from the code it replaced because both died the same way. Bump it with
 * every change that alters behaviour.
 */
const BUILD = "2026-09-08b";

function json(body: unknown, status = 200) {
  return new Response(JSON.stringify(body), {
    status,
    headers: {
      "Content-Type": "application/json",
      "Cache-Control": "no-store",
      "X-Grader-Build": BUILD,
    },
  });
}`);
s = s.replace(`if (!claimed) return json({ status: "grading", attempt }, 202);`, `if (!claimed) return json({ status: "grading", attempt, build: BUILD }, 202);`);
if (s === before) { console.error("NO CHANGE"); process.exit(1); }
if (crlf) s = s.replace(/\n/g, "\r\n");
fs.writeFileSync(p, s); console.log("patched");
EOF
deno check supabase/functions/grade-rrc-section/index.ts 2>&1 | sed 's/\x1b\[[0-9;]*m//g' | grep -c "TS2345"; git add supabase/functions/grade-rrc-section/index.ts && git -c core.safecrlf=false commit -q -m "Stamp grade-rrc-section responses with a build marker

Lovable deploys edge functions on push with no visible receipt. The
signed-URL fix and the code it replaced both died with
WORKER_RESOURCE_LIMIT, so which one was running could not be told from
outside. The 202 body and every response header now carry the build.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>" && git push -q origin main && date -u && git rev-parse --short HEAD
[/external_agent_tool_call]

Here is the text for Lovable, then the status on Celia.

**Paste this into Lovable:**

```text
Read, Recall, Colour: how the drill works and how it is graded

The drill itself does not change. Get the explanation and the grading right.

What she does, one stimulus at a time

1. A stimulus appears. She reads it to herself, silently. Nothing is said yet.
2. She looks away from the text and says line one out loud, in her own words. Then line two, and so on, one line at a time, until every line is done. She can glance back at the text between lines.
3. She presses Space. She gets one last scan of the full text.
4. She presses Space again. The text disappears. She restates the whole stimulus out loud from memory, in her own words.
5. It goes straight to the next stimulus. No pause, no feedback, nothing judged yet.

The whole section is one run: 20 or more stimuli, one unbroken recording, one clock.

What is captured

- Everything she says out loud is transcribed with timestamps.
- Every stretch of speech is tied to the stimulus that was on screen while she said it, and within that stimulus, to the line she was on. The whole-thing recall after the second Space is its own stretch.
- So for each stimulus we hold: her line-by-line rephrasing, her full recall, and the audio for each.

How it is graded

- Nothing is graded during the run. Grading happens once, at the very end, when she submits the finished section. All stimuli are graded in that one pass.
- The AI grader compares each stimulus against the golden standard already in our database for that section: the stored standard recording and its transcript.
- The bar is "same points, same quality", not the same words. Her phrasing can differ; personal variance is fine. Full marks means every point the standard makes is carried, at the same level of quality. Reading the line back word for word does not count as a rephrase.
- For each stimulus the grader marks what she carried, what she dropped, and names the big misses. Then an overall report card for the section.

The grader must be able to do this for every stimulus in the section on its own, with clear per-question feedback and one overall verdict.
```

```text
Read, Recall, Colour: how the drill works and how it is graded

The drill itself does not change. Get the explanation and the grading right.

What she does, one stimulus at a time

1. A stimulus appears. She does not read it through. She reads only the first line, silently, in her head. Nothing is said aloud while she is looking at the text.
2. She looks away from the screen and says that line out loud, in her own words, without the text in view. She never reads a line aloud; every spoken line is a rephrase from memory.
3. She comes back to the text, reads the next line silently, looks away, and rephrases it out loud. One line at a time, read in her head, said out loud while looking away, until the last line is done. The stimulus stays on screen the whole time.
4. She presses Space once. The text disappears. She restates the whole stimulus out loud from memory, in her own words.
5. It goes straight to the next stimulus. No pause, no feedback, nothing judged yet.

The whole section is one run: 20 or more stimuli, one unbroken recording, one clock.

What is captured

- Everything she says out loud is transcribed with timestamps.
- Every stretch of speech is tied to the stimulus that was on screen while she said it, and within that stimulus, to the line she had just read. The whole-thing recall after Space is its own stretch.
- She reads silently and only speaks while looking away, so the recording has a gap before every line. The line split keys off those speech gaps plus line order; it must not assume continuous talking.
- So for each stimulus we hold: her rephrase of each line, her full recall, and the audio for each.

How it is graded

- Nothing is graded during the run. Grading happens once, at the very end, when she submits the finished section. All stimuli are graded in that one pass.
- The AI grader compares each stimulus against the golden standard already in our database for that section: the stored standard recording and its transcript.
- The bar is "same points, same quality", not the same words. Her phrasing can differ; personal variance is fine. Full marks means every point the standard makes is carried, at the same level of quality. Saying a line back word for word is not a rephrase and does not count.
- For each stimulus the grader marks, line by line, what she carried and what she dropped, then does the same for the full recall, and names the big misses. Then an overall report card for the section.

The grader must be able to do this for every stimulus in the section on its own, with clear per-question feedback and one overall verdict.
```

## assistant

The critique already ran this session on the section briefing page, with two isolated passes (a design review with live desktop inspection, plus the automated detector). Full report is in [wiki/RRC-BRIEFING-CRITIQUE-2026-09-08.md](wiki/RRC-BRIEFING-CRITIQUE-2026-09-08.md). Here is what it found.

**Score: 27 out of 40.** Acceptable, upper end. Structure is right; the misses are content gaps and vocabulary drift, not layout.

**Does it look AI-made? No.** Single column, four numbered steps, one primary action, Fraunces on cream. No gradient text, side stripes, hero metrics, icon grids, or em dashes. The detector returned zero findings on the page and its stylesheet.

**What's working**

- A page instead of a modal, with all four steps open and numbered. The copy is the best on the platform.
- The hero styles are honestly re-grounded for paper, with comments saying why.
- Accessibility basics hold, measured live: heading order H1 H2 H2 H3, Back is 44x44, 2px focus rings.

**The two problems that matter (P1)**

- "One take, about 25 minutes, no pause" is not on the page anywhere. The code comment says Step 4 carries it. It doesn't; the sentence lives only in a rules array nothing renders, and the "twenty-five minutes" heading is inside the collapsed essay. That is the exact fact she needs at the moment she presses Start.
- The calming notice ("Nothing is recording yet...") is styled identically to the four step cards, so the one sentence meant to lower her shoulders reads as a fifth thing to do.

**Smaller (P2/P3)**

- Step body lines run about 105 characters; cap at 62ch.
- Three different micro-label styles on one page, and the two H2s render at different weights, so the "Read the long version" heading looks like a stray sentence.
- The essay's lede is silently overridden to body size by a more specific rule.
- The Start button keeps its landing-page hover lift and shadow on a task page.

**Persona flags:** a first-timer gets a microphone permission dialog with no warning; on mobile the reassurance sits 1,000px above the Start she actually presses; a client at 9pm can't tell how long this is or whether she can stop.

**Recommended order:** `clarify` (put the one-take sentence beside the foot Start), `layout` (un-box the notice), `typeset` (measure, labels, H2s, lede), `quieter` (the button), then `polish`.

Say which of these you want and I'll do them. Note the deploy problem still stands: page changes will push and publish, but that path worked for the frontend last session, so these are safe to ship.

/* The hero classes are written for the door's dark purple ground: .sl-title is
   white at up to 96px, .sl-tagline is white with a drop shadow. This page sits
   on paper, so both were being drawn in white on cream. The briefing page uses
   the same classes for the same copy, so it restates the colour and the size
   here rather than forking them. */
/* The focus ring, per control rather than once at the top.
   `.sl :focus-visible` sets a ring for the whole world, and loses the cascade on
   the two button classes, so Start and Back matched :focus-visible and computed
   outline-style: none. A keyboard student could not see where she was on the one
   control that begins the run, and Back had no indicator at all.

   The colour depends on the ground, and these buttons appear on both: the door
   draws them on the dark hero panel, every other surface on cream. */

.sl .sl-brief:focus-visible {
  outline: 2px solid var(--rd-ink);
  outline-offset: 3px;
}
.rd-hero .sl-start:focus-visible,

  outline-color: var(--rd-cream);
}

.sl-brief {

}

import { useEffect, useRef } from "react";
import type { SectionPhase } from "./section";

/**
 * One quiet element that moves with her voice, and the only evidence on screen
 * that the microphone is alive.
 *
 * For twenty-one minutes she talks into a blank card. The recorder already
 * reports a REFUSED microphone, but it cannot see a live-but-silent one -- a
 * muted input, a device the OS switched underneath her, a headset that dropped
 * off. That failure produces a perfect-looking run, a clean upload, and a
 * Report Card of zeros, and she finds out at the end of the twenty-one minutes.
 *
 * So this does two jobs with one reading.
 *
 * **Presence.** A bar that answers "is anything reaching it" without answering
 * "how am I doing". Deliberately not a level meter with a target: nothing here
 * may look like a score, because nothing is judged until the section ends.
 *
 * **The fault.** If the input never leaves the noise floor across a whole
 * stimulus, that is said out loud at the boundary, while there is still a
 * section left to save -- and unsaid the moment anything is heard again.
 *
 * Nothing here re-renders the runner. The bar is written to as a DOM node
 * inside an animation frame, because a component that set state at 60Hz would
 * be re-rendering the drill room underneath her while she reads.
 */

/**
 * The line between "an open microphone in a quiet room" and "nothing arriving".
 *
 * RMS over the raw time-domain samples, so it is amplitude and not loudness. A
 * genuinely dead input sits at or near zero; a live one in a silent room still
 * carries the room. Set low on purpose: this exists to catch nothing at all,
 * never to judge a quiet speaker, and a false "your microphone is dead" during
 * a real run is worse than the fault it reports.
 */
const NOISE_FLOOR = 0.004;

export function SectionStage({
  stimulus,
  phase,
  stimulusNumber,
  totalStimuli,
  error,
  onExit,
  elapsedSeconds,
  getStream,
  onDeadInput,
  micRefused,
  onRetryMic,
  confirmingExit,
  onConfirmExit,
  onCancelExit,
  countdown = null,
  onAdvance,
  coach = null,
  onDismissCoach,
}: SectionStageProps) {
  const noKeyboard = useNoKeyboard();
  // Position across the section, drawn as the rail's fill. Progress inside the
  // current stimulus is deliberately not shown: the app does not know it.
  const through = Math.min(stimulusNumber - 1, totalStimuli) / Math.max(totalStimuli, 1);

  return (
    <div className="ac-section">
      {/* The white rail, as on the real section: chrome above the paper, never
          on it. It carries where she is and the way out, and nothing else. */}
      <header className="ac-section-head">
        <span className="ac-section-where">
          Stimulus <b>{stimulusNumber}</b> of {totalStimuli}
        </span>
        <div className="ac-section-rail" aria-hidden>
          <span className="ac-section-rail-fill" style={{ transform: `scaleX(${through})` }} />
        </div>
        {/*
          * Elapsed, counting UP, against the goal.
          *
          * This screen carried no clock at all, on the reasoning that a running
          * clock produces the rushed reading the drill exists to treat. The
          * drill is against the clock, though, and a student with no way to see
          * the time cannot pace a 25-minute section. Counting up rather than
          * down is the compromise: she can see where she is without a number
          * falling towards zero in the corner of her eye.
          */}
        {elapsedSeconds !== undefined && (
          <span className="ac-section-clock" aria-live="off">
            {clock(elapsedSeconds)}
          </span>
        )}
        {onExit ? (
          <button type="button" className="ac-section-exit" onClick={onExit}>
            Leave
          </button>
        ) : (
          <span />
        )}
      </header>

      {/*
        * Leaving throws the run away, so it says so first.

            </div>

          {/* The card's own footer, where the real section keeps its controls.
              What she is doing sits left, the one key that does it sits right. */}
          <div className="ac-section-foot">
            <p className="ac-section-instruction" role="status">
              {countdown !== null
                ? micRefused
                  ? "The section has not started."
                  : "Say something to check your microphone."
                : INSTRUCTION[phase]}
              {getStream ? (

                warning leads, because it is the new information. */}

function patch(p, edits) {
  let s = fs.readFileSync(p, "utf8"); const crlf = s.includes("\r\n"); s = s.replace(/\r\n/g, "\n");
  for (const [a, b] of edits) { if (!s.includes(a)) { console.error("MISSING in " + p + ":\n" + a.slice(0, 120)); process.exit(1); } s = s.replace(a, b); }

}

import { render, screen } from "@testing-library/react";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { SectionStage } from "./SectionStage";
import type { SectionStimulus } from "./section";

/*
 * The one thing she asked to be able to see mid-run: that it is recording.
 * A word in the rail, not only a red dot, so it survives grayscale and a
 * screen reader. Absent, not merely hidden, when nothing is being recorded.
 */

const stimulus: SectionStimulus = {
  slug: "pt-134-s3-q07",
  chunks: [{ n: 1, text: "The maintenance logs do not establish what the curators assumed." }],
} as SectionStimulus;

function renderStage(recording: boolean) {
  return render(
    <SectionStage
      stimulus={stimulus}
      phase="working"
      stimulusNumber={7}
      totalStimuli={26}
      error={null}
      elapsedSeconds={461}
      recording={recording}
    />,
  );
}

describe("the recording mark in the rail", () => {
  it("says Recording, in words, while the microphone is open", () => {
    renderStage(true);
    const mark = screen.getByRole("status", { name: "" });
    expect(mark.textContent).toMatch(/recording/i);
    expect(mark.className).toContain("ac-section-rec");
  });

  it("says nothing when nothing is being recorded", () => {
    renderStage(false);
    expect(screen.queryByText(/^recording$/i)).toBeNull();
  });
});
EOF

description: Commit the page and runner changes and push through safe-push
command: git add src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionBriefingPage.tsx src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionBriefingPage.test.tsx src/features/activity-centre/components/section-launch.css src/features/activity-centre/recall/SectionStage.tsx src/features/activity-centre/recall/SectionStage.recording.test.tsx src/features/activity-centre/recall/SectionRunner.tsx src/features/activity-centre/recall/section.css && git -c core.safecrlf=false commit -q -F - <<'EOF'
Say the one-take fact where she presses Start, and mark Recording in the rail

The briefing page's critique found two things that mattered. The fact she
needs at the moment she presses Start, that it runs in one take and does
not pause, was on the page nowhere: a code comment claimed Step 4 carried
it, and Step 4 did not. It is said at the foot now, beside the Start she
presses after reading, with twenty-five minutes named as the goal she is
working toward rather than a length, and the authored closer rendered as
the last beat before Start. The calming notice shared fill, hairline and
radius with the four step cards, so it read as a fifth thing to do; it is
a plain line in ink now.

Smaller: the step prose gets a 62ch measure; the section headings and step
numbers share the file's one mono label style; the long-version summary
gets the same heading treatment and a drawn chevron instead of a " +";
the lede is no longer overridden by the essay's paragraph rule; the Start
button loses its landing-page lift and shadow on this task page; the head
is stacked on purpose instead of by accident; the redundant chip is gone.

In the runner, a "Recording" mark with a pulsing dot sits in the rail for
the whole run, in words so it survives grayscale and a screen reader.
Reduced motion stops the pulse.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF

Source element: <body>
---
READ, RECALL, COLOUR
Read, Recall, Colour · PT21 Section 3

Read it, tell it back, react to it. Then the stimulus disappears and you say the whole argument back from memory.

Start the section
Back

Nothing is recording yet. The microphone opens when you press Start.

WHAT YOU DO, STEP BY STEP
STEP 1
Read it.

Read about one sentence at a time, silently. Give each word enough attention to hear it naturally in your head. If that feels like too much to hold, take a shorter piece. When you take on too much, the part that slips is always the beginning.

STEP 2
Tell it back.

Look away from the screen and tell it back in your own words, out loud, like you are telling a friend what it said. Out of order is fine. Casual is fine. Laughing is fine.

STEP 3
Colour it.

React. A doubt, a question, an example, a joke, a feeling. One or two seconds is plenty. It does not have to be clever, it has to be yours.

STEP 4
The Recall.

Press space. The stimulus disappears and does not come back. Now say the entire argument back from memory, out loud, with every detail you can still hold. This is the part that gets scored.

Read the long version

When you are ready, press Start. The clock begins counting up and the recording begins.

Start the section
