## assistant

[external_agent_tool_result]
.task: Work through the stimulus out loud, a piece at a time. When you have got all of it, press space. The text goes away, and you say the entire argument back from memory.
.coach.drill: a whole Logical Reasoning section run out loud, where the student reads each line of a stimulus, looks away, tells it back from memory in her own words, reacts to it, and gives The Recall of the whole stimulus before moving to the next one
.coach.rubric[0]: Judge by meaning, never by wording. Her own words are required here, so a good recall will never match the line's phrasing. High overlap with the original is the failure, not the standard.
.coach.rubric[1]: Order is free. She is encouraged to deliver the ideas out of the order she read them in, and reordering must never cost her anything.
.coach.rubric[2]: Casual, unpolished, laughing delivery is expected. Never mark down for style, length or fluency.
.coach.rubric[3]: A detail merely gestured at is not carried. Naming the topic is not the same as saying what was claimed about it.
.coach.rubric[4]: Numbers, names, dates, quantities and connecting words are each their own detail. Never treat one as carried because the sentence around it was.
.coach.rubric[5]: A reaction that could be pasted onto any other sentence does not count as a colour. Judge whether it engages this content, never whether it is correct or well put.
.coach.rubric[6]: Score The Recall against the captured key for that stimulus, not against a retelling. A different name for the same move is fine; missing the move is not.
.coach.rubric[7]: Silence during a silent read is correct and expected. Only silence inside a recall or a colour counts against her.
.rules[0]: Everything here is out loud. The whole section records in one take, and it never pauses.
.rules[1]: Read silently, about a sentence at a time. When you are not sure how much to take on, take less.
.rules[2]: Look away and tell it back in your own words. Then react to it. Both out loud.
.rules[3]: Keep going like that through the whole stimulus.
.rules[4]: Press space when you have got all of it. The stimulus vanishes and stays gone. Now say the whole argument back from memory, with every detail you can hold.
.rules[5]: Press space again and the next one loads. No breaks, no going back, all the way to the end.
.tagline: Read it, tell it back, react to it. Then the stimulus disappears and you say the whole argument back from memory.
.briefing.lede: Worth reading properly once. After that, skim the rules above and go.
.briefing.closer: One a day holds you steady. The second one is where you improve.
.briefing.sections[0].body[0]: You follow a stimulus, sentence by sentence, and it all makes sense as it goes. Then you get down to answer choice C and realise you are holding almost none of it. That is not a comprehension problem. It is a retention problem, and it is the one this drill was built for.
.briefing.sections[0].body[1]: Understanding is necessary for everything and sufficient for nothing. If the argument is not still with you while you work the answers, your understanding never got the chance to help.
.briefing.sections[0].body[2]: Most of what you call reading is built for getting to the end. Scanning names. Dropping the connecting words. Turning a list of three into one label. That is a fine way to read a phone bill. It is a bad way to read something that is about to ask you for every detail back.
.briefing.sections[0].heading: You can understand every sentence and still arrive with nothing.
.briefing.sections[1].body[0]: Three steps through the stimulus, then a fourth step once you have read all of it. You decide how much to take on at a time, and nothing on the screen is tracking where you are.
.briefing.sections[1].body[1]: Space is the only key, and you press it twice per stimulus. The first press takes the text away and asks you for the whole argument from memory. The second press moves you to the next stimulus. Nothing advances on its own, so a paus...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

function patch(p, edits) {
  let s = fs.readFileSync(p, "utf8"); const crlf = s.includes("\r\n"); s = s.replace(/\r\n/g, "\n");
  for (const [a, b] of edits) { if (!s.includes(a)) { console.error("MISSING in " + p + ":\n" + a.slice(0, 160)); process.exit(1); } s = s.replace(a, b); }
  if (crlf) s = s.replace(/\n/g, "\r\n"); fs.writeFileSync(p, s); console.log("patched", p);
}

`/* The page arrives in reading order: head, notice, then each step a beat

  from {
    opacity: 0;
    transform: translateY(10px);
  }
  to {
    opacity: 1;
    transform: translateY(0);
  }
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-head,
.sl-briefing-page .sl-notice,

.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-more,

}

.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-more,
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-foot {
  animation-delay: 640ms;
}
@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {

  .sl-briefing-page .sl-notice,
  .sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-h2,
  .sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-list li,

  .sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-foot {
    animation: none;
  }
  .sl-bp-more > summary::after {
    transition: none;
  }
}
@media (max-width: 640px) {
  .sl-bp-head {
    flex-direction: column;
  }
}`],

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_result]
Assess and improve typography that feels generic, inconsistent, or poorly structured — turning default-looking text into intentional, well-crafted type.

---

## Register

Brand: run the font selection procedure in [brand.md](brand.md). Pairing follows the brand's lane (display serif + sans body for editorial/luxury, one committed sans for tech, etc.). Fluid `clamp()` scale, ≥1.25 ratio between steps.

Product: system fonts and familiar sans stacks are legitimate here. One well-tuned family typically carries the whole UI. Fixed `rem` scale, 1.125–1.2 ratio between more closely-spaced steps.

---

## Assess Current Typography

Analyze what's weak or generic about the current type:

1. **Font choices**:
   - Are we using invisible defaults? (Inter, Roboto, Arial, Open Sans, system defaults)
   - Does the font match the brand personality? (A playful brand shouldn't use a corporate typeface)
   - Are there too many font families? (More than 2-3 is almost always a mess)

2. **Hierarchy**:
   - Can you tell headings from body from captions at a glance?
   - Are font sizes too close together? (14px, 15px, 16px = muddy hierarchy)
   - Are weight contrasts strong enough? (Medium vs Regular is barely visible)

3. **Sizing & scale**:
   - Is there a consistent type scale, or are sizes arbitrary?
   - Does body text meet minimum readability? (16px+)
   - Is the sizing strategy appropriate for the context? (Fixed `rem` scales for app UIs; fluid `clamp()` for marketing/content page headings)

4. **Readability**:
   - Are line lengths comfortable? (45-75 characters ideal)
   - Is line-height appropriate for the font and context?
   - Is there enough contrast between text and background?

5. **Consistency**:
   - Are the same elements styled the same way throughout?
   - Are font weights used consistently? (Not bold in one section, semibold in another for the same role)
   - Is letter-spacing intentional or default everywhere?

**CRITICAL**: The goal isn't to make text "fancier" — it's to make it clearer, more readable, and more intentional. Good typography is invisible; bad typography is distracting.

## Plan Typography Improvements

Consult the [typography reference](typography.md) for detailed guidance on scales, pairing, and loading strategies.

Create a systematic plan:

- **Font selection**: Do fonts need replacing? What fits the brand/context?
- **Type scale**: Establish a modular scale (e.g., 1.25 ratio) with clear hierarchy
- **Weight strategy**: Which weights serve which roles? (Regular for body, Semibold for labels, Bold for headings — or whatever fits)
- **Spacing**: Line-heights, letter-spacing, and margins between typographic elements

## Improve Typography Systematically

### Font Selection

If fonts need replacing:
- Choose fonts that reflect the brand personality
- Pair with genuine contrast (serif + sans, geometric + humanist) — or use a single family in multiple weights
- Ensure web font loading doesn't cause layout shift (`font-display: swap`, metric-matched fallbacks)

### Establish Hierarchy

Build a clear type scale:
- **5 sizes cover most needs**: caption, secondary, body, subheading, heading
- **Use a consistent ratio** between levels (1.25, 1.333, or 1.5)
- **Combine dimensions**: Size + weight + color + space for strong hierarchy — don't rely on size alone
- **App UIs**: Use a fixed `rem`-based type scale, optionally adjusted at 1-2 breakpoints. Fluid sizing undermines the spatial predictability that dense, container-based layouts need
- **Marketing / content pages**: Use fluid sizing via `clamp(min, preferred, max)` for headings and display text. Keep body text fixed

### Fix Readability

- Set `max-width` on text containers using `ch` units (`max-width: 65ch`)
- Adjust line-height per context: tighter for headings (1.1-1.2), looser for body (1.5-1.7)
- Increase line-height slightly for light-on-dark text
- Ensure body text is at least ...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

description: Apply the type scale, run the page tests, commit and push
command: node - <<'EOF'
const fs=require("fs");const p="src/features/activity-centre/components/section-launch.css";
let s=fs.readFileSync(p,"utf8");const crlf=s.includes("\r\n");s=s.replace(/\r\n/g,"\n");
const anchor=`@media (max-width: 640px) {
  .sl-bp-head {
    flex-direction: column;
  }
}`;
if(!s.includes(anchor)){console.error("anchor");process.exit(1);}
s=s.replace(anchor,`/* ---- the briefing page's type scale ----
   Sizes on this page had drifted to 16, 16.5, 17, 18, 19 and 20px: six steps
   where the eye can tell apart three. One scale, in rem so it follows her
   browser setting, ratio about 1.2 between neighbours:
     label 0.6875  body 1  lead 1.1875  step 1.375  title clamp(2, 3.4vw, 3)
   Roles: mono for labels, Inter for anything she reads at length, Fraunces
   for the lines that are spoken to her (title, step names, lede, closer). */
.sl-briefing-page {
  --bp-label: 0.6875rem;
  --bp-body: 1rem;
  --bp-lead: 1.1875rem;
  --bp-step: 1.375rem;
  font-size: var(--bp-body);
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-title {
  font-size: clamp(2rem, 3.4vw, 3rem);
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-tagline {
  font-size: var(--bp-lead);
  line-height: 1.5;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-notice,
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-fact {
  font-size: var(--bp-lead);
  line-height: 1.5;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-h2,
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-n {
  font-size: var(--bp-label);
}
/* Step names in the display face: they are the four things said to her,
   and the sans bold they were in made them read as form labels. */
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-list li strong {
  font-family: var(--rd-display);
  font-weight: 500;
  font-variation-settings: "opsz" 24, "SOFT" 30, "WONK" 0;
  font-size: var(--bp-step);
  line-height: 1.2;
  letter-spacing: -0.01em;
  margin-bottom: 8px;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-list li p {
  font-size: var(--bp-body);
  line-height: 1.65;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-foot p {
  font-size: var(--bp-body);
  line-height: 1.6;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-closer {
  font-size: var(--bp-step);
  line-height: 1.3;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-essay p {
  font-size: var(--bp-body);
  line-height: 1.7;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-essay p.rd-lede {
  font-size: var(--bp-step);
  line-height: 1.4;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-essay h3 {
  font-size: var(--bp-step);
  margin: 32px 0 10px;
}
` + anchor);
if(crlf)s=s.replace(/\n/g,"\r\n");fs.writeFileSync(p,s);console.log("patched");
EOF

Source element: <body>
---
Read, Recall, Colour · PT21 Section 3

Read it, tell it back, react to it. Then the stimulus disappears and you say the whole argument back from memory.

Start the section
Back

Nothing is recording yet. The microphone opens when you press Start.

WHAT YOU DO, STEP BY STEP
STEP 1
Read it.

Read about one sentence at a time, silently. Give each word enough attention to hear it naturally in your head. If that feels like too much to hold, take a shorter piece. When you take on too much, the part that slips is always the beginning.

STEP 2
Tell it back.

Look away from the screen and tell it back in your own words, out loud, like you are telling a friend what it said. Out of order is fine. Casual is fine. Laughing is fine.

STEP 3
Colour it.

React. A doubt, a question, an example, a joke, a feeling. One or two seconds is plenty. It does not have to be clever, it has to be yours.

STEP 4
The Recall.

Press space. The stimulus disappears and does not come back. Now say the entire argument back from memory, out loud, with every detail you can still hold. This is the part that gets scored.

READ THE LONG VERSION

It runs in one take and does not pause. Twenty-five minutes for the whole section is the goal you are working toward, not a limit.

One a day holds you steady. The second one is where you improve.

When you are ready, press Start. Recording begins the moment you do, and the timer starts with it. The first time, your browser will ask to use your microphone. Allow it.

Start the section

import { openDB, type IDBPDatabase } from "idb";

/**
 * A crash-proof copy of a section recording, on the student's own machine.
 *
 * A Read, Recall, Colour section is twenty minutes of one unbroken recording,
 * and until this existed all twenty lived in the tab's memory until the moment
 * she finished. A crash, a closed lid or a flat battery at minute nineteen took
 * every second of it, and nothing anywhere knew it had ever happened.
 *
 * So the recorder now runs a SECOND time over the same microphone, emitting a
 * piece every few seconds, and the pieces land here as they are made. Nothing
 * is uploaded during the run: this is her disk, not the network, so it costs no
 * bandwidth and cannot stall her.
 *
 * ## Why a second recorder rather than chunking the first
 *
 * Measured in Chrome, on the real product:
 *
 *   no timeslice   1 piece    duration 5.88s   seeks with 0ms error
 *   timeslice      6 pieces   duration Infinity   cannot seek at all
 *
 * MediaRecorder writes the duration into the file when it stops. Ask it for
 * pieces as it goes and the header has already been emitted, so the duration
 * can never be filled in and the browser reports `Infinity`. Clips are cut out
 * of the single recording by seeking into it (ADR 0020), so chunking the
 * primary recorder would have quietly broken Clips to protect against a crash.
 *
 * The primary recorder is therefore left exactly as it was, and this is a
 * parallel copy that only has to be good enough to GRADE: Deepgram reads the
 * sound itself, and the stimulus boundaries are the app's own millisecond
 * stamps rather than anything read out of the container.
 *
 * ## Order is the whole correctness story
 *
 * Only the FIRST piece a MediaRecorder emits carries the EBML header; the rest
 * are bare clusters. Reassembled out of order the file does not begin with a
 * header and is not a WebM file at all. So every piece carries its sequence
 * number, writes are serialised per run, and assembly sorts before joining.
 *
 * ## Everything here is best-effort
 *
 * Private windows, full disks and browsers with storage disabled all make
 * IndexedDB throw. A backup that cannot be written must never take down the
 * run it exists to protect, so every function swallows its own failures and
 * the drill continues without it.
 */

const DB_NAME = "ac-recording-backup";
const DB_VERSION = 2;
const CHUNKS = "chunks";
const RUNS = "runs";
const ROWS = "rows";

/** Anything older than this was abandoned long ago and is only taking up room. */
export const STALE_AFTER_MS = 7 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000;

/** What a recovered recording needs to know about itself to be uploaded. */
export interface BackupRun {
  runId: string;

----
export async function beginBackup(run: BackupRun): Promise<void> {
  active.add(run.runId);
  if (!available()) return;
  try {
    await (await db()).put(RUNS, { ...run, liveUntil: Date.now() + LIVE_GRACE_MS });
  } catch (e) {
    console.error("[rrc] could not open the local recording backup", e);
  }
}
----
export async function putChunk(runId: string, seq: number, blob: Blob): Promise<void> {
  if (!available()) return;
  await serialised(runId, async () => {
    try {
      const chunk: Chunk = {
        key: `${runId}:${String(seq).padStart(6, "0")}`,
        runId,
        seq,
        bytes: await toArrayBuffer(blob),
      };
      const instance = await db();
      await instance.put(CHUNKS, chunk);
      // The heartbeat. A piece just landed, so whoever is recording this run
      // is still here; say so where every tab can read it.
      const run = (await instance.get(RUNS, runId)) as BackupRun | undefined;
      if (run) await instance.put(RUNS, { ...run, liveUntil: Date.now() + LIVE_GRACE_MS });
    } catch (e) {
      // A full disk or a private window. The run continues; the primary
      // recording is unaffected and is still the real one.
      console.error("[rrc] could not keep a local copy of a recording chunk", e);
    }
  });
}
----
export async function assemble(run: BackupRun): Promise<Blob | null> {
  if (!available()) return null;
  try {
    const chunks = (await (await db()).getAllFromIndex(CHUNKS, "runId", run.runId)) as Chunk[];
    if (!chunks.length) return null;
    chunks.sort((a, b) => a.seq - b.seq);
    return new Blob(chunks.map((c) => c.bytes), { type: run.mimeType });
  } catch (e) {
    console.error("[rrc] could not reassemble a recovered recording", e);
    return null;
  }
}
[/external_agent_tool_result]

import fs from "node:fs"; import path from "node:path"; import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";
const REPO = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "..");
function readEnv(file: string): Record<string, string> { const out: Record<string, string> = {}; if (!fs.existsSync(file)) return out; for (const line of fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8").split(/\r?\n/)) { const t = line.trim(); if (!t || t.startsWith("#")) continue; const i = t.indexOf("="); if (i < 0) continue; out[t.slice(0, i).trim()] = t.slice(i + 1).trim().replace(/^["']|["']$/g, ""); } return out; }
const env = { ...readEnv(path.resolve(REPO, "../../.env")), ...readEnv(path.join(REPO, ".env")) };
async function main() {
  const { error } = await supabase.auth.signInWithPassword({ email: env.LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL, password: [REDACTED] }); if (error) throw error;
  const p = "749e2eae-7969-464f-bf96-a4d4cfafe2bf/3afbbbde-07df-41cf-871f-5106624c2988.webm";
  const { data, error: e } = await supabase.storage.from("drill-recordings").createSignedUrl(p, 600);
  if (e) throw e;
  const out = process.env.OUT!;
  const res = await fetch(data.signedUrl); if (!res.ok) throw new Error("fetch " + res.status);
  fs.writeFileSync(out, Buffer.from(await res.arrayBuffer()));
  console.log("saved", fs.statSync(out).size);
}
main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });
EOF

const fs=require("fs");
const rms=fs.readFileSync("rms.txt","utf8").split("\n").filter(l=>l.includes("RMS_level")).map(l=>parseFloat(l.split("=")[1]));
const wins=[];for(const line of fs.readFileSync("windows.txt","utf8").trim().split("\n")){for(const t of line.trim().split(/\s+/)){const m=t.match(/^([wr])(\d+)-(\d+)$/);if(m)wins.push({phase:m[1],s:+m[2],e:+m[3]});}}
const loud=t=>{const v=rms[Math.floor(t)];return v==null||!isFinite(v)?null:v};
function score(a,b){let rw=[],rr=[];for(const w of wins){const s=w.s*a+b,e=w.e*a+b;let sum=0,n=0;for(let t=s;t<e;t+=1){const v=loud(t);if(v!=null){sum+=v;n++}}if(!n)continue;(w.phase==="r"?rr:rw).push(sum/n)}const mean=x=>x.reduce((p,c)=>p+c,0)/x.length;return {a,b,work:mean(rw).toFixed(1),recall:mean(rr).toFixed(1),gap:(mean(rr)-mean(rw)).toFixed(2)}}

// overall loudness by 10-min block

let best=null;for(let a=1.0;a<=1.85;a+=0.01){for(const b of [0,60,300,600,1200,1800,2200]){const r=score(a,b);if(!best||+r.gap>+best.gap)best=r}}
console.log("best scale/offset by recall-vs-working loudness gap:",best);

`const CLOCK_MIN_SHARE_IN_WINDOW = 0.5;

/**
 * The least share of words inside windows for a found shift to be trusted.
 * Higher than the plain proof's floor on purpose: a shift is a claim about the
 * whole file, and a weak fit is a wrong claim dressed as a repair.
 */
const CLOCK_ALIGN_MIN_SHARE = 0.8;

/** How far the file's clock may lead the app's before alignment gives up. */
const CLOCK_ALIGN_MAX_OFFSET_MS = 4 * 60 * 60 * 1000;

/**
 * Words with a start at or after \`fromMs\` and before \`toMs\`, from a list
 * sorted by start. Two binary searches, so a scan over thousands of candidate
 * shifts stays cheap.
 */
function countStartsBetween(starts: number[], fromMs: number, toMs: number): number {
  let lo = 0, hi = starts.length;
  while (lo < hi) { const mid = (lo + hi) >> 1; if (starts[mid] < fromMs) lo = mid + 1; else hi = mid; }
  const first = lo;
  hi = starts.length;
  while (lo < hi) { const mid = (lo + hi) >> 1; if (starts[mid] < toMs) lo = mid + 1; else hi = mid; }
  return lo - first;
}

function shareInside(starts: number[], segments: Segment[], offsetMs: number): number {
  if (!starts.length) return 0;
  let inside = 0;
  for (const seg of segments) inside += countStartsBetween(starts, seg.startMs + offsetMs, seg.endMs + offsetMs);
  return inside / starts.length;
}

/**
 * Where the app's window clock sits inside the file, found from the words.
 *
 * On 2026-09-08 a 66-minute section arrived in a 116-minute file: the recorder
 * had been running for 37 minutes before the run's clock started, the words
 * sat 2,200 seconds later than every window said, and the proof refused the
 * run. The windows partition the run, so the shift that puts the most words
 * inside them is the shift the file actually has. This scans every half
 * second from zero to the observed drift and hands back the best fit, and the
 * caller decides whether the fit is good enough to trust.
 */
function alignClock(words: Word[], segments: Segment[]): { offsetMs: number; share: number } {
  const starts = words.map((w) => w.start * 1000).sort((a, b) => a - b);
  const spanEndMs = Math.max(0, ...segments.map((s) => s.endMs));
  const lastWordMs = starts.length ? starts[starts.length - 1] : 0;
  const maxOffset = Math.min(CLOCK_ALIGN_MAX_OFFSET_MS, Math.max(0, lastWordMs - spanEndMs) + 10 * 60 * 1000);
  let best = { offsetMs: 0, share: shareInside(starts, segments, 0) };
  for (let offset = 500; offset <= maxOffset; offset += 500) {
    const share = shareInside(starts, segments, offset);
    if (share > best.share) best = { offsetMs: offset, share };
  }
  // Refine to the tenth of a second around the best half second.
  for (let offset = best.offsetMs - 450; offset <= best.offsetMs + 450; offset += 100) {
    if (offset < 0) continue;
    const share = shareInside(starts, segments, offset);
    if (share > best.share) best = { offsetMs: offset, share };
  }
  return best;
}`);
// 2. the proof: try alignment before refusing

    const spanEndMs = Math.max(0, ...allSegments.map((s) => s.endMs));
    const lastWordMs = words.length ? words[words.length - 1].end * 1000 : 0;
    const inAnyWindow = words.filter((w) => allSegments.some((s) => w.start * 1000 >= s.startMs && w.start * 1000 < s.endMs)).length;
    const clock = {
      words: words.length,
      words_inside_a_window: inAnyWindow,
      span_end_ms: spanEndMs,
      last_word_ms: Math.round(lastWordMs),
      drift_ms: Math.round(lastWordMs - spanEndMs),
    };`,
`    let allSegments = responses.flatMap((r) => ((r.rrc as Record<string, unknown>).audio_segments ?? []) as Segment[]);
    const spanEndMs = Math.max(0, ...allSegments.map((s) => s.endMs));
    const lastWordMs = words.length ? words[words.length - 1].end * 1000 : 0;
    const countInside = (segments: Segment[]) =>
      words.filter((w) => segments.some((s) => w.start * 1000 >= s.startMs && w.start * 1000 < s.endMs)).length;
    let inAnyWindow = countInside(allSegments);

    /*
     * If the windows and the words disagree, look for the shift before
     * refusing. A found shift is applied to every window on every row, so
     * everything downstream (the words per stimulus, dead air, the clip spans
     * a Report Card seeks with) reads the file where the speech actually is.
     * It is written onto the grade so a shifted run is never mistaken for a
     * clean one.
     */
    let alignment: { offset_ms: number; share_before: number; share_after: number } | undefined;
    if (words.length > 0 && inAnyWindow / words.length < CLOCK_ALIGN_MIN_SHARE) {
      const found = alignClock(words, allSegments);
      if (found.offsetMs > 0 && found.share >= CLOCK_ALIGN_MIN_SHARE) {
        alignment = {
          offset_ms: found.offsetMs,
          share_before: Number((inAnyWindow / words.length).toFixed(3)),
          share_after: Number(found.share.toFixed(3)),
        };
        for (const r of responses) {

            ...seg,
            startMs: seg.startMs + found.offsetMs,
            endMs: seg.endMs + found.offsetMs,
          }));
        }
        allSegments = responses.flatMap((r) => ((r.rrc as Record<string, unknown>).audio_segments ?? []) as Segment[]);
        inAnyWindow = countInside(allSegments);
        console.warn("[grade-rrc-section] window clock shifted to fit the file", { assignmentId, ...alignment });
      }
    }

    const clock = {
      words: words.length,
      words_inside_a_window: inAnyWindow,
      span_end_ms: spanEndMs,
      last_word_ms: Math.round(lastWordMs),
      drift_ms: Math.round(lastWordMs - spanEndMs),
      ...(alignment ? { alignment } : {}),
    };`);
